teljes csere, nem patch: `kind`/`setIndex`/`exerciseId`/`target*` nem változik, warmup soron a `rir` mindig `null`; újraderivált `medals`-szal a válaszban) + **`DELETE /api/train/workouts/{id}/sets/{setId}`** (`mezo-l3on` — soft delete, majd az adott gyakorlat megmaradt szettjeinek `setIndex`-e 0..n-1-re rendeződik, mert a FE pozicionális kurzora `logged.length`-ként hézagmentességet feltételez; mindkét új végpont csak `active` instance-on él — **409 `TRAIN_WORKOUT_NOT_ACTIVE`** máskülönben —, egy skip-marker sorra vagy idegen/ismeretlen `setId`-re **404**), `POST .../{id}/skip` (F3 — records a skip-marker set for a whole exercise), `POST .../{id}/feedback` (upsert per exercise), `POST .../{id}/finish`, `PUT /api/train/exercises/{exerciseId}/note` (F4 — owner-scoped durable note write, ≤500 chars). Cross-day ITs: `CrossDayWorkoutIT`.
- **`GET /api/train/workouts?from&to` (date-range summaries — `mezo-t16y.1`, added for the Insights-Weekly composition):** returns owner-scoped **completed** workout instances (`status = 'completed'` — the done-semantics shift of `mezo-cd8s`, see the done-state subsection below; **was** ≥1 non-skipped set) whose date falls in the **inclusive** `[from, to]` range, **date-ascending**. `WorkoutService.listWorkouts` (a pure read, no `@Transactional`) validates `from ≤ to` — `from>to` → **400 `TRAIN_INVALID_DATE_RANGE`** — and **reuses the existing `WorkoutSessionRepository.findDoneInstancesBetween`** query (the same entities finder the companion `get_training_log` tool uses under `scope=gym`, §5), so **no new repository query** was added. FE client: `trainApi.listWorkouts(from, to)` (`GET /api/train/workouts?from=&to=`); its original consumer, `useWeekly` (`data/insights/weeklyHooks.ts`), was **retired with the Insights Weekly tab (`mezo-p2tr`)** — the op itself survives, now consumed instead by `useWeekWorkouts` (`data/train/workoutDetailHooks.ts`) for the current week's range. See [`insights.md`](insights.md) §2.2 for the retirement. **`.../{id}/finish` now returns an optional `levelUp`** (the gamified-progression payload): `WorkoutService.finishWorkout` computes + attaches it inside its `@Transactional`, but **only when the `mezo.feature.progression.enabled` switch is on** (gated by the `ProgressionGate` `@ConditionalOnProperty` bean, injected via `ObjectProvider`). Start/resume responses never carry it — the shared `toInstanceResponse` is untouched. See the progression backend doc (`_platform-api-backend.md` §4e); the FE now **captures** this `levelUp` and presents it via the shared `LevelUpProvider` host + `LevelUpScreen` overlay (P5, §2 active-workout COMPLETE phase + the sport/run log flows).
- **DTOs:** `WorkoutTodayResponse` (now also carries **`completedWorkout`**, a nullable `WorkoutInstanceResponse` = the most recent `completed` instance of today's template day dated **today**; drives the Kész/Megnézem hero, the day-sheet gating and the session-route redirect without a second fetch — `mezo-cd8s`), `TodayExercise` (now carries the recipe fields `warmupSets`/`workingSets`/`repMin`/`repMax`/`targetRIR`/`anchorWeightKg` instead of `sets`/`targetReps`, plus optional `prescribedSets`/`rationale`), **new `PrescribedSet` (`{kind: warmup|working, targetWeightKg?, targetReps, targetRIR?}`)**, **new `ProgressionSignal` (`{lever: weight|rep|hold|deload, deltaKg?, deltaReps?, targetWeightKg?, targetReps, rationale}` — Progressive Overload Plan 1, `mezo-5pfe`)** + **new `OverloadSummary` (`{weightUp, weightDown, repUp, hold}`, day-level — `weightUp` counts WEIGHT-lever increases only, `weightDown` the reductions)**, `LastWeekRef`, `WorkoutStartRequest`, `WorkoutInstanceResponse`, `WorkoutSummaryResponse` (`{id, date, status}` — the `listWorkouts` range summary; `status` enum `planned|active|completed|skipped`), **`WorkoutDetailResponse` (`{id, templateSessionId, date, status, title, dayLabel, durationEst, exercises: WorkoutDetailExercise[]}`) + `WorkoutDetailExercise` (`{exerciseId, name, muscle, type, warmupSets, workingSets, repMin, repMax, targetRIR, skipped, sets: ExerciseSetResponse[]}`) — the done-day review payload (`mezo-cd8s`)**, `ExerciseSetResponse` (now `+ kind`), `SetLogRequest` (now `+ kind`, default `working`; **`rir` is optional since `mezo-eerq`** — the FE omits it on warmup sets, persisting a NULL instead of a fake RIR 0), **new `SetUpdateRequest` (`{weightKg, reps, rir?, side?, note?}` — the `updateSet`/`deleteSet` full-replace body, `mezo-l3on`)**, `WorkoutSkipRequest`, `WorkoutFeedbackInput`, `ExerciseNoteRequest` (`note` ≤500, nullable to clear). The meso-day `GymExercise`/`GymExerciseInput` DTOs likewise carry the recipe fields (replacing `sets`/`targetReps`), so the planner/builder round-trips the recipe. `TodayExercise.note` surfaces the durable note on read; `saveExerciseNote` is owner-scoped (foreign/missing exercise → **404**, over-length note → **400**). `logSet` validation: setIndex 0-49, weightKg 0-999, reps 1-100, rir 0-5 (optional), side `^[LBR]$`, note ≤500. `logSet`/`skipExercise` on a completed instance → **409** (`TRAIN_WORKOUT_NOT_ACTIVE`); a foreign exercise → **404**. `skipExercise` persists an `ExerciseSetEntity` with `skipped=true`, the next free `setIndex` for that exercise, and null performance fields — a skip marker is **not** a logged set. **Since `mezo-cd8s` the gym done-state is `status = 'completed'`**, so neither a skip marker nor any number of logged sets flips a day to done on its own — only the explicit finish (or the lazy auto-close of a stale instance carrying ≥1 non-skipped set) does (see the done-state subsection below).
- "Last week" comparison: `WorkoutService.lastWeekRefs` picks the top working set of the most-recent completed session in which the exercise's **identity** (catalog id, else exact name — `ExerciseHistoryResolver`, soft-deleted day-edit rows included) was trained — **since `mezo-eq4w` this is identity-resolved, not row/template-day-scoped**, so a day edit's soft-delete+reinsert row swap never blanks the comparison. **Warmup sets (`kind='warmup'`) are excluded** from this pick (working-set history only).

#### Set recommendation engine — hypertrophy drive + progressive overload (`mezo-dhdr` + `mezo-5pfe`)
`SetRecommendationService.prescribe(createdBy, ex, deloadWeek)` (`feature/train/service/SetRecommendationService.java`) computes the per-set targets that `getToday` attaches as `prescribedSets`, and — since Progressive Overload Plan 1 (`mezo-5pfe`) — the structured `progression: ProgressionSignal` per exercise. It resolves the exercise's **top working set** of the most recent completed session where the exercise's **identity** was trained (catalog id, else exact name — the shared `ExerciseHistoryResolver`, `mezo-eq4w`; formerly template-day + row-scoped, which a day edit's row swap silently reset), then **delegates the lever/weight/rep decision to a new pure `ProgressionDecider`** (`service/ProgressionDecider.java` — no Spring, no DB; unit-tested directly via `ProgressionDeciderTest`). The **RIR-aware, deload-aware double-progression matrix** (SUPERSEDES the earlier reps-only rule of "reps < rep_min → drop the increment; reps in range → hold"): `slack = loggedRIR − targetRIR` (a **null logged RIR is treated as on-target**, `slack = 0`, neutral — an un-rated set never triggers a false grind-hold or a false pass).
  - **Deload week** (see below) → **regress** regardless of reps/RIR: ~0.9× the last working weight (plate-snapped), reps reset to `rep_min`.
  - `reps ≥ rep_max` → **+weight**: bump by the type increment (plate-snapped), reps reset to `rep_min`.
  - `rep_min ≤ reps < rep_max` → **+rep** (build toward `rep_max`) — **unless** the last set was a **grind** (`slack < 0`, i.e. the logged RIR came in under the target), in which case the engine **holds/consolidates** the same weight instead of adding a rep.
  - `reps < rep_min` → **−weight** (drop by the increment, plate-snapped) when the athlete was **at or over target effort** (`slack ≤ 0`); otherwise **hold** — the load was too heavy but the effort wasn't there either, so the engine waits rather than moving the wrong way.
  - **Per-machine weight memory (`mezo-bk7l2`).** A WEIGHT/DELOAD target that lands on a weight KNOWN to be missing on the exercise's machine is moved by the pure `WeightSnapper` onto the nearest available weight, with reps recomputed at equal effort (Epley round-trip incl. the target RIR — the Java twin of the FE `repEquivalence.ts`, same 20 % guard). Candidates: weights ever logged on the exercise's identity (`ExerciseHistoryResolver.workingWeightsEverLogged` — proven to exist) first, the plate grid around the base (±4 steps) only as fallback. **The move keeps its direction relative to last session's reference weight** — an upward step never snaps back onto the reference, which would re-prescribe the same set forever (Liftosaur #338) — so `deltaKg` is never zero and the day tally is untouched. Of the lighter/heavier candidate, the one whose reps sit inside `[rep_min, rep_max]` wins; both/neither → the smaller overshoot, ties to the lighter. The rationale gains ` · 65 kg nincs a gépen → 62.5 kg`; working sets, warmups (from the new base) and `progression.targetWeightKg/targetReps/deltaKg` follow. **Learning, no UI (owner decision 2026-09-24):** `logSet` receives the engine's ORIGINAL slot weight as `SetLogRequest.prescribedWeightKg` (the FE sends it beside the swap-adjusted `targetWeightKg`); a working set logged NEAR it — within `max(type increment, mezo.hypertrophy.gap-near-fraction (0.10) × prescribed)` — records the prescribed weight in `exercise_weight_gap` (`WeightGapService`, keyed by exercise identity, never the unstable template row id); a bigger swap is a choice and teaches nothing. `logSet`/`updateSet` at a weight **heal** it (soft delete) — the memory corrects itself. All of it runs only with the hypertrophy-drive switch on. One gym only (per-gym memory is a deferred idea).

  On the **first session** (no working-set history) it still seeds from `anchor_weight_kg` (or leaves the target **null** with no anchor) exactly as before, and `progression` is **null** — there is nothing to compare against yet. **Weightless/bodyweight history** (a logged set with no weight) still progresses on reps only (`progression.lever = 'rep'`, unchanged behavior). **Plyometric / bodyweight exercises are weightless** — the engine returns null target weights (reps/RIR only), which the FE renders as a reps-only stepper. Warmup sets are generated by ramping the working target down through the configured `warmupRamp` — and are **emitted even when the base weight is unknown** (first session, no anchor; `mezo-eerq`): their target weight is simply null, keeping the prescribed list aligned with the recipe's set count so the FE never loses the `B{n}` labels / RIR suppression on a first session. The method returns a `Prescription(sets, rationale, progression)` record (`service/Prescription.java`, now 3 fields) — the set list, the human-readable `rationale` shown in the active workout, and the structured signal the FE renders as `ProgressionBanner`/the prep chips (§2). `WorkoutService.getToday` tallies each exercise's `progression.lever` into the day-level `overloadSummary`: WEIGHT tallies by the sign of `deltaKg` into `weightUp`/`weightDown`, REP → `repUp`, HOLD/DELOAD → `hold`. It reads **completed-instance history only**, so a live (open) session never feeds back into its own prescription → the recommendation is **stable across a mid-workout resume** (§9).
- **Deload-week detection** (`WorkoutService.getToday`, `mezo-5pfe`; index fixed by `mezo-hi9m` DA1): `deloadWeek` is derived from `activeMeso.getPhaseCurve().get(activeMeso.getCurrentWeek() - 1)` equalling `"Deload"` (case-insensitive) — **`currentWeek` is 1-based, `phaseCurve` is 0-indexed**, so the lookup is `currentWeek - 1` (`WorkoutService.java:172-177`; a pre-existing off-by-one against a 0-based index was corrected here). **Bound-checked and null-safe** — no active meso, no phase curve, or `currentWeek - 1` outside `[0, phaseCurve.size())` all resolve to **not deload** rather than throwing, so a meso whose `currentWeek` has drifted past its own curve length degrades gracefully instead of 500ing. `VolumeProgressionService.isDeloadPhase` applies the same corrected 1-based lookup independently (see below).
- **Config** (`mezo.hypertrophy.*` → `HypertrophyProperties`, `feature/train/config/HypertrophyProperties.java`): `plateStep` (2.5 — rounding granularity for computed kg, also used by the deload 0.9× snap), `defaultIncrement` (2.5 — fallback), a per-type `increment` map (compound 5.0 / isolation 2.5), `defaultWarmupSets` (2 — a backend-side fallback, independent of the FE picker's own type-seeded default in `libraryToGymExercise`, below), and **`warmupLadders`** (`Map<Integer, List<Ramp(pct, reps)>>`, budget wave-2 `mezo-dnln` — SUPERSEDES the earlier single flat `warmupRamp` list): **count-keyed, absolute-rep** ladders, one per `warmupSets` count — `1: [70%×4]`, `2: [50%×8, 75%×3]`, `3: [50%×8, 70%×4, 90%×2]` (`application.yml` `mezo.hypertrophy.warmup-ladders`). `SetRecommendationService.warmupLadder(n)` looks up `warmupLadders.get(n)` for `n ≤ 3`; for `n > 3` it **prepends `(n-3)` repeats of the 3-ladder's first rung** (50%×8) ahead of the full 3-ladder, so a 4th/5th warmup set never invents a new percentage — it just repeats the lightest rung. `@Validated` record, wired in `application.yml` under `mezo.hypertrophy:` — never `@Value`.
- **FE adaptive warmup-count suggestion (client-only, `mezo-dnln`):** `suggestedWarmupSets(day, exId)` (`frontend/src/features/train/logic/warmupSuggest.ts`) is a pure heuristic — no server call — that picks a sane `warmupSets` count for an exercise from its type/muscle-group and its position among the day's earlier non-plyo exercises of the same **budget group** (`setBudget.ts`'s `budgetGroup`, see `#### Weekly bands` below): `type === 'plyo'` or **bodyweight-ish** (`anchorWeightKg == null && repMin ≥ 15` — the gate is the range's floor, not its ceiling, so a weighted-feel 12-15 isolation under a cut-prep/recovery preset scheme, which has no anchor yet on first add, still gets ramp-up; only a range that's high-rep **throughout** [e.g. 15-20] is bodyweight-ish enough to skip warmups, `mezo-szsi`) → 0; unknown budget group (e.g. a sport row) → 0; the first non-plyo `compound` opening its group → 3, but 2 when `anchorWeightKg != null && anchorWeightKg < 60` (a lighter compound needs less ramp-up); a later `compound` of an already-hit group → 1; an `isolation` opening its group → 1; an `isolation` after the group was hit → 0 ("group hit" only counts non-plyo exercises earlier in the day's order — a plyo row never makes a later exercise "not first"). Consumed two ways in `MesoEditor.tsx`: (1) passed as `suggestedWarmup` to every `ExerciseAccordionRow`, which shows a `↺ javaslat: {n}` chip in the expanded Bemelegítő tile when it differs from the stored count (tap → `onChange({ warmupSets: n })`); (2) the **add-path override** — `MesoEditor`'s existing new-exercise-detection `useEffect` (the one that auto-expands a freshly added row) ALSO fires `onChange(day.day, newId, { warmupSets: suggestion })` once, when the suggestion differs from `libraryToGymExercise`'s type-seeded default (compound 2 / isolation 1 / plyo 0, `mezo-dq60` — no longer a flat `warmupSets: 2` for every pick) — so a newly picked exercise lands on its adaptive count without the athlete tapping the chip. Because the picker already seeds a type-aware value close to what `suggestedWarmupSets` would land on anyway, this override is usually a no-op in practice — it's a safety net for the cases where position/anchor-weight context (which `libraryToGymExercise` doesn't see) pulls the count the other way. In real mode this is a **second PUT** right after the add's own persist (`MesoExercises.persistDay` fires on every `onChange`, `MesoExercises.test.tsx`).
- **Switch:** the whole engine is gated by `mezo.feature.hypertrophy-drive.enabled` via the `HypertrophyDriveGate` `@ConditionalOnProperty` marker bean (`feature/train/HypertrophyDriveGate.java`; key constant `FeaturesConfiguration.HYPERTROPHY_DRIVE_SWITCH`). Off → `WorkoutService` skips the engine and returns null `prescribedSets`/`rationale`/`progression`, and `overloadSummary` is null, so the FE logs ad-hoc with no progression UI at all.
- **Downstream consumer (Progressive Overload Plan 3, `mezo-gj42`):** the deterministic
  `OverloadChallengeGenerator` (proactive-owned) reads this same per-exercise `progression` off
  `getToday` to pick the day's biggest jump for a proactive `overload` challenge — no new engine logic
  here; see [proactive.md §HBWI](proactive.md).

#### Volume progression — weekly per-muscle set ramp (Progressive Overload Plan 2, `mezo-hi9m`)
`VolumeProgressionService.rolloverIfDue(createdBy, activeMeso)` (`feature/train/service/VolumeProgressionService.java`) is called as one of the first steps of `WorkoutService.getToday` (right after the active meso is resolved, before the deload check and the effective-set distribution below — `WorkoutService.java:119-121`), gated by an `ObjectProvider<VolumeProgressionGate>` (present only when `mezo.feature.volume-progression.enabled=true`, **default on**, mirroring `HypertrophyDriveGate`/`ClosingBlockGate`). It performs the **weekly rollover**: a real write path for what used to be seed-only `currentSets`/`volumeRecompute`.
- **Priority tiers resolve the ramp ceiling per muscle group (`mezo-3m5m`, spec GD4):** `PriorityTier.of(meso.getMusclePriorities(), muscle)` (`feature/train/service/PriorityTier.java`) reads the meso's sparse `muscle_priorities` map — a `null` map, an absent key, or an unrecognized value all resolve to **Grow**, the default — and `tier.ceiling(mev, mav, mrv)` picks which volume landmark is the weekly ramp's "100%":

  | Tier | Ceiling | Ramps toward it? |
  |---|---|---|
  | **Emphasize** | MRV | yes — same ceiling as every muscle had before `mezo-3m5m` |
  | **Grow** (default/absent key) | MAV | yes — **was MRV before `mezo-3m5m`** |
  | **Maintain** | MEV | only up to MEV — below it (e.g. right after a deload) it RAMPs back up like any other tier; at/above MEV it HOLDs |

  **This is an intentional behaviour change, not a regression:** before `mezo-3m5m` every tracked muscle ramped all the way to MRV; now a group left on the default Grow tier ramps only to MAV, and only a group explicitly marked **Emphasize** (FE-capped at `EMPHASIZE_CAP` = 2 groups at once) keeps the old MRV ceiling. **Early-deload detection and the deload floor are UNCHANGED by tier** — `VolumeDecider`'s `earlyDeload = prevSets >= mrv && grind` and the deload floor `⌈mev/2⌉` both read the row's RAW `mrv`/`mev`, never the tier ceiling, so a Grow group capped at MAV never early-deloads off its (lower) ceiling — it still has to reach the raw MRV first. **A mid-cycle tier switch never cuts current sets:** switching a group to Maintain only lowers its ceiling to MEV — since `currentSets` is normally already at/above MEV at the point of a mid-cycle switch, the next rollover HOLDs there rather than dropping it to MEV; only an actual deload-phase week reduces sets, and that check is tier-independent too. **There is no separate ramp-disable flag for Maintain** — the ceiling alone encodes the tier: `VolumeDecider`'s RAMP condition is simply `prevSets < rampCeiling` (plus the shared productive/no-grind checks), so a Maintain muscle deloaded below MEV recovers back up to MEV over subsequent weeks exactly like a Grow/Emphasize muscle ramps toward its own ceiling — it just never ramps PAST MEV.
- **Idempotent per calendar week:** `MesoWeeks.clampWeek(startDate, weeks)` (a shared, package-visible `startDate`-anchored 1-based week helper, also used by the arc endpoint below) computes the week containing today; if it hasn't advanced past `meso.volumeRecompute.lastRun` ("W{n}"), the call is a no-op — safe to invoke on every `getToday`.
- **Otherwise, per `MuscleGroupVolumeLogEntity` row** (one per muscle group on the meso): it reads the **previous** calendar week's COMPLETED instances, collapses each logged exercise's 21-token zone to its coarse **`MuscleGroup`** (`service/MuscleGroup.java` — pure, `chest`/`back`/`shoulder`/`biceps`/`triceps` are dash-prefix-collapsed from the finer zones; `quad`/`ham`/`glute`/`calf`/`core` and legacy coarse tokens pass through unchanged), and derives per-group `loggedLastWeek` (working-set count) + `grind` (any exercise whose most-recent logged working set landed ≥ `grindRirGap` RIR below its `targetRir` — the recovery-proxy signal, since the engine has no direct cross-load input yet); both signals are computed only over exercises with `countsTowardVolume = true` (`VolumeProgressionService.lastWeekSignals`) — the fix-zárás closing block and plyometrics never move the rollover. It then resolves each row's `PriorityTier` (see the bullet above) and runs **`VolumeDecider.decide`** (`service/VolumeDecider.java` — pure, Spring-free, unit-tested via a dedicated test): week 1 → `MEV`; deload week or already-at-raw-`MRV`-and-grinding → `round(prevSets × deloadFraction)` floored at `⌈mev/2⌉`; productive AND below the tier's ceiling (logged ≥ target, not grinding, `prevSets < rampCeiling`) → `+step`; else → **HOLD** (for a Maintain group this HOLDs at/above MEV, but still RAMPs back up toward MEV first if a deload left it below). The result is persisted onto `currentSets` + appended as a `ProvenanceEnvelope.Adjustment` (`kind: "pattern"`) on the row's existing `source` — the baseline MEV/MAV/MRV is untouched, only a new adjustment is appended.
- The mesocycle's `currentWeek` advances to the new calendar week and `volumeRecompute` gets a fresh `{lastRun: "W{n}", nextRun: "W{n+1}", trigger: "weekly-rollover", changes: [...]}` — `changes` is a terse per-muscle `{muscle, change, reason, deload}` list (e.g. `"+2 sets"` / `"tartás"` / `"deload"`), NOT the richer free-text prose the mock/seed fixtures carry (be honest about that gap when describing this to a reader expecting mock-parity prose).
- A muscle group with no volume-log row on the meso is simply skipped, never fabricated.
- **Baseline seeding — where the rows are born (`mezo-xlmp`, tier-aware since the mesocycle wizard redesign):** volume-log rows are no longer demo-seed-only. `VolumeProgressionService.seedBaselines(createdBy, mesoId, priorities)` inserts one row per coarse `MuscleGroup` the meso's TEMPLATE days actually train — counting only exercises with `countsTowardVolume = true`, so a template whose only chest work is an exempt exercise seeds no chest row — from the fixed **`mezo.volume.baselines`** RP table (chest 8/14/20 · back 10/16/22 · shoulder 8/12/18 · biceps 6/10/14 · triceps 6/10/14 · quad 8/12/18 · ham 6/10/14 · glute 8/12/18 · calf 6/10/16; core/traps intentionally absent — RP treats their MEV as ~0), with **`currentSets = PriorityTier.of(priorities, group).weekOneStart(mev, mav, mrv)`** — MEV+2 for an EMPHASIZE group (capped at MRV), plain MEV otherwise, so the freshly seeded row already agrees with what `MesoPlanSkeleton`/`MesoPlanGeneratorService` put in week 1 of a generated plan (the W1 rollover stamps its own START audit on top regardless) — and a provenance of an `RP guidelines · intermediate` baseline, confidence 0.5 (contract-required on `VolumeSource`), no adjustments. Called from `TrainService.createMesocycle` (**ACTIVE-status creates only** — a planned meso stays profile-less, so `MesoVolume`'s "csak aktív mesocikluson" guard stays true, `src.musclePriorities()` passed through) and **unconditionally from `TrainService.activateMesocycle`** (idempotent — an existing row's group is skipped untouched — so tapping activate doubles as the backfill path for pre-`mezo-xlmp` mesos created without rows; `target.getMusclePriorities()` passed through). Gated by the same `VolumeProgressionGate` as the rollover (switch off ⇒ byte-identical pre-seed behavior). A group present in the template but absent from the config table (core, the `lats`/`rear-delt` legacy pass-throughs, sport rows) is never fabricated — DA5 holds on the create path too. ITs: `VolumeBaselineSeedIT` (5 tests incl. the create→`getToday` W1-MEV-distribution chain) + `VolumeBaselineSeedSwitchOffIT` + **`MesoStartTierSeedIT`** (the tier-aware MEV+2 start on an EMPHASIZE group, mesocycle wizard redesign).
- **Effective per-exercise working sets (same `getToday` call, DA6):** once the rollover has run (or on a week where it was already up to date), and only when the switch is on and the meso has volume-log rows, `WorkoutService.effectiveWorkingSets` distributes each muscle group's `currentSets` across **the meso template week's** same-group exercises that carry `countsTowardVolume` — proportional to each exercise's template `workingSets`, remainder to the largest, never below 1 — and this **derived** count overrides `TodayExercise.workingSets` (the template row itself is untouched). Distributing per DAY (the pre-`mezo-gbo7` behaviour) multiplied weekly volume by training frequency and broke the spec's `[MEV, MRV]` bound. Exempt exercises — the fix-zárás closing block and plyometrics — keep their template `workingSets` and are excluded from the rollover signals, the arc's actuals and baseline seeding. This effective count is what's threaded into the hypertrophy-drive engine: `SetRecommendationService.prescribe(createdBy, ex, deloadWeek, effectiveWorkingSets)` — the 4-arg overload, the only caller being `getToday`. Switch off, or no volume-log rows on the meso → every exercise keeps its template `workingSets` unchanged (byte-identical to pre-Plan-2 behavior).
- **Per-date lighten overlay (S5, `mezo-d58h.5`) — a READ-TIME adjustment, never a template edit.** The advice card's `lighten_tomorrow` action ([proactive.md](proactive.md) §9 decision ll) writes a `workout_day_adjustment (created_by, date, set_delta)` row for tomorrow's date, `set_delta` bounded `-3..0` by a CHECK constraint. `WorkoutService.getToday` looks the row up for the resolved day ONCE and applies its `setDelta` to `effectiveWorkingSets` **AFTER** the muscle-group volume distribution above and **BEFORE** the `SetRecommendationService.prescribe` call — `effective = Math.max(1, effective + dayDelta)`, never below one working set. The order matters: the distribution uses each exercise's own template `workingSets` as its weighting, so subtracting the delta any earlier would skew the whole muscle group's split for that day rather than trimming the one exercise the card meant to lighten. It never touches `meso_template`/`exercise` — gym exercises hang off the weekday template row with no per-instance override, and the only existing template-write path (a day edit) re-creates exercise rows with new UUIDs, which would both lighten every future occurrence of that weekday AND orphan already-logged `exercise_set` rows. The two companion prose paths (`TrainTools`, `ContextSnapshotAssembler`) apply the same `workingSets + dayDelta` read independently, so the coaching prose never disagrees with what the workout screen shows. **`getWorkoutDetail` (the past-day review) deliberately does NOT apply it** — a review of an already-logged day must show what was actually prescribed that day, not today's un-related overlay. `workout_day_adjustment` is in `ResetDatabase`'s TRUNCATE list.
- **Config** (`mezo.volume.*` → `VolumeProperties`, `feature/train/config/VolumeProperties.java`, `@Validated` record): `step` (2 — sets added on a productive week), `deloadFraction` (0.5 — deload/at-MRV-grind target as a fraction of the previous week's sets), `grindRirGap` (2 — RIR-below-target gap counted as a grind), and **`baselines`** (`Map<String, Baseline(mev, mav, mrv)>` keyed by coarse muscle group — the RP landmark table the create/activate seeding reads, `mezo-xlmp`; see the baseline-seeding bullet above).
- **Switch:** `mezo.feature.volume-progression.enabled` (`FeaturesConfiguration.VOLUME_PROGRESSION_SWITCH`, default **true**) via the `VolumeProgressionGate` marker bean (`feature/train/VolumeProgressionGate.java`).
- **Read-only companion — the volume-arc endpoint:** `GET /api/train/mesocycles/{id}/volume-arc` (`operationId getMesocycleVolumeArc`, `TrainController.getMesocycleVolumeArc`) → `VolumeArcService.arc` (`feature/train/service/VolumeArcService.java`) returns `MesocycleVolumeArcResponse { mesocycleId, title, currentWeek, weeks, startDate, endDate, status, phaseCurve[], muscles: MuscleVolumeArc[] }`; each `MuscleVolumeArc { muscle, region, mrv, weeks: VolumeArcWeek[] }` (`region` = a FE color-family key — coral/sky/lav/rose/sage/amber — resolved from a fixed `MuscleGroup → region` table); each `VolumeArcWeek { week, phase, planned, actual, isCurrent }` where `planned` is a scaffold projection (week 1 = MEV, deload weeks = `round(ceiling × deloadFraction)`, else ramps by `step` up to the muscle's **tier-resolved ceiling** — same `PriorityTier` resolution as the rollover above (Emphasize MRV / Grow MAV / Maintain MEV, `mezo-3m5m`) — independent of what `VolumeDecider` actually decided that week; the response's own `MuscleVolumeArc.mrv` field always reports the row's RAW `mrv` untouched by tier — only this internal scaffold ceiling shifts) and `actual` is the aggregated logged **working**-set count per `MuscleGroup` per `startDate`-anchored meso-week (a new `ExerciseSetRepository.aggregateWorkingSetsByMuscleAndDate` query, bucketed via `MesoWeeks.weekOf`, and joined on `countsTowardVolume = true` so exempt exercises never inflate the actuals) — **`null` for any week beyond `currentWeek`** (nothing logged yet). This endpoint is **read-only, ownership-filtered via `OwnershipGuard`, and deliberately NOT gated** by the volume-progression switch (a harmless read that never mutates state) — it renders even if the rollover itself is switched off. FE consumption: §2 `Mezociklus áttekintő`.

#### Plan generator (mesocycle wizard redesign, `mezo-d20.7.9`)
`POST /api/train/meso-plans/generate` (`MesoPlanGenerateRequest {daysOfWeek[2..6 of Hét..Vas], weeks 4..8, priorities?{group: emphasize|grow|maintain}, goalText?}` → `MesoPlanGenerateResponse {draftId, template: MesoTemplateUpsertRequest, rationale, llmUsed}`) is the single-model Hypertrophy generator; nothing is persisted — the FE posts `template` back to `createMesoTemplate` unchanged. **`draftId` (mezo-76f6) is a backend-minted uuid, one fresh id per generate/regenerate call, no content hash** — the FE echoes it back to `POST /api/ai-drafts/{draftId}/outcome` (see [`companion.md`](companion.md) §4 "AI draft outcomes") when the proposal is accepted/edited/discarded; this generator runs deterministically as often as not (the LLM pick step is optional and can contribute zero picks), so its outcome rows are the reason the admin scorecard's row-set union had to widen beyond `llm_log_history`/feature-map keys (see that same section). Pipeline, all in `service/`: **`MesoPlanSkeleton`** (pure) derives the split from the day COUNT (2–3 Full · 4 Upper/Lower · 5 Upper/Lower/Push/Pull/Legs · 6 PPL×2 — every coarse group ≥2×/week by construction), week-1 sets from `PriorityTier.weekOneStart` (EMPHASIZE MEV+2, else MEV) and ceilings from `PriorityTier.ceiling`, spreads the weekly amount over the group's days (remainder earliest), and emits the phase curve (`MEV…MAV…MRV, Deload`); **`MesoPlanFiller`** (pure) fills each (day, group) frame with 1–2 catalog exercises (compound-first, stim-desc, rotating on the second weekly occurrence) — the fallback that always exists; the train-owned **`MesoPlanLlm`** port (companion's `MesoPlanLlmAdapter`, `[meso-plan]` marker, SMART tier, gated by `mezo.feature.meso-plan-ai.enabled` + the companion switch) lets Gemini pick exercises INTO the frames from the candidate list; **`MesoPlanMerger`** (pure) validates every pick (known id, right group, real day), caps accepted picks per (day, group) the same way `MesoPlanFiller` does (2 when the frame has ≥6 sets else 1, further bounded by the tunable + valid-pick count), and renormalizes sets to the frame — the model never changes volume. `MesoPlanMerger.merge` returns a `MergeResult{days, appliedPicks}`; `MesoPlanGeneratorService` reports **`llmUsed = true` only when at least one LLM pick was accepted** (`appliedPicks > 0`) — an answer that changed nothing (e.g. the fake's default `{"days":[]}`) is treated exactly like no answer: the deterministic days AND the deterministic rationale are kept. Tunables: `mezo.meso-plan.*` (`MesoPlanProperties`: 2 exercises/group/day, compound 8–10 / isolation 12–15 reps, RIR 1) — every group ≥2×/week and ≤8 sets/session hold by construction of the split table (asserted in `MesoPlanSkeletonTest`), not by a tunable. **Seeding is tier-aware since this slice:** `seedPlanBaselines`/`seedBaselines` start an EMPHASIZE group's `currentSets` at MEV+2 so the volume log and the generated program agree in week 1.

Two contract quirks worth knowing when reading `MesoPlanGenerateRequest`/`-Response`: the `pattern: '^(emphasize|grow|maintain)$'` on `priorities` map values is **not enforced by the generated DTO** (a plain `Map<String, String>` — OpenAPI map-value patterns don't reach MapStruct/Jackson validation) — an invalid tier is instead caught server-side by `PriorityTier.normalize` inside `MesoPlanGeneratorService.generate`, 400ing with `TRAIN_MUSCLE_PRIORITY_TIER_INVALID`; and `daysOfWeek`'s `uniqueItems: true` generates as a **`Set<String>`**, not a `List`, so `MesoPlanGeneratorService` re-sorts it into `MesoPlanSkeleton.DAY_ORDER` order itself rather than trusting request order.

#### Weekly bands — planning-time weekly set-band layer (client-only, `mezo-7rdg` + `mezo-d20.14`)
A **separate, FE-only layer from the volume-progression engine above** (D4 in the original design spec, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-01-set-budget-unified-editor-design.md`): it warns the athlete **while planning** a mesocycle, on every render, with no server call and no touch of `muscle_group_volume_log`/MEV/MAV/MRV data. Source: Jeremy Ethier / Built With Science ("What is the fastest way to gain 20 lb of muscle naturally?", yt `ehQ_5TThkRI`), drawing on Dr. Mike Zourdos' set-volume + proximity-to-failure meta-analyses and Jake Remmert's per-session set-cap finding. **The wizard v2 redesign (`mezo-d20.14`) retired the old percent-pill `SetBudgetCard` entirely** — `frontend/src/features/train/logic/weeklyBands.ts`'s `weeklyBands(days, priorities, landmarks?)` and `components/WeeklyBandsCard.tsx` are the sole surviving planning-time UI everywhere `SetBudgetCard` used to render (the wizard's `Program` step, the builder's `MesoEditor`, and `MuscleWeekSheet`'s read-only Gym mirror) — no percentages shown as text anywhere, no MEV-floor "under" signal, no optimal-zone underlay: each row is just `{group, label, tier, planned, start, ceiling, pct, step}`, rendered as a **`start → ceiling`** number pair (or `{planned} szett · tart` for Maintain) with a tier chip and a `▲ +2 / hét` / `plafonon` step chip; `pct` (`planned / ceiling`) only ever drives a bar's fill width, never rendered as text.
- **Style-from-RIR — no new field (D1):** `setStyle(targetRIR)` (`frontend/src/features/train/logic/setBudget.ts`) classifies every exercise straight from the existing `targetRIR` — `≤1` → **failure 🔥** (sets to failure), `≥2` → **volume 🌿** (2-3 reps short). The `MesoEditor`/`ExerciseAccordionRow` segmented toggle just writes RIR **0** or **2**; manual RIR fine-tuning re-classifies automatically.
- **`weeklyBands` itself:** one `BandRow` per coarse **budget group** (`budgetGroup(muscle)` — `chest/back/traps/shoulder/biceps/triceps/quad/ham/glute/calf/core`; off-day keys `''`/`'sport'` excluded) that carries `countsForVolume` working sets this week (`ex.workingSets` summed, exempt sets simply not counted — `weeklyBands` tracks no separate `exemptSets` field, unlike `daySessionBreakdown` below). `tier = tierOf(priorities, group)` (`logic/musclePriorities.ts`; Maintain/Grow/Emphasize); `start = weekOneSets(tier, lm)` and `ceiling = ceilingSets(tier, lm)` (both `logic/mesoPlan.ts` — Emphasize starts `MEV+2` and ramps to `MRV`, Grow `MEV → MAV`, Maintain holds at `MEV`); the landmark `lm` source is, in order, an explicit `volumePerMuscle[group]` (the mesocycle's own live `{mev,mav,mrv}`, when the caller has it) → else the static **`GROUP_LANDMARKS`** table (`setBudget.ts` — a FE mirror of the backend's `mezo.volume.baselines` RP table: chest 8/14/20 · back 10/16/22 · shoulder 8/12/18 · biceps 6/10/14 · triceps 6/10/14 · quad 8/12/18 · ham 6/10/14 · glute 8/12/18 · calf 6/10/16); a group with **no** landmark at all (`traps`/`core`) is simply **omitted** from the rows — there is no sets-only fallback row any more. Rows sort ceiling-desc (Emphasize/high-ceiling groups read first), then label.
- **The original fatigue-cap formula is retired from the UI but the pure function survives, for one caller only.** `budgetOf(failureSets, volumeSets) = failureSets/FAILURE_WEEKLY_CAP(12) + volumeSets/VOLUME_WEEKLY_CAP(20)` and its supporting table `GROUP_MEV`/`muscleBudgets`/`BudgetLevel` (incl. the retired `'under'` level and the `zoneStart` MEV-floor projection) still live in `setBudget.ts` and are still exported, but **nothing in the planning UI reads them any more** — `weeklyBands` does not call `muscleBudgets` at all. The **sole remaining consumer is `logic/weekZone.ts`** (the live done-vs-plan layer, below), which still projects `WeekZoneRow.doneBudget`/`todayBudget`/`planBudget` on the old fatigue scale and still keys its `below`/`entering`/`in`/`over` status off `GROUP_MEV`. **This is a known, deliberate gap, not an oversight — Train Titanium T12 built the whole Terhelés reface on this same fatigue scale rather than closing it** (the `Heti` subsection's "two-scales decision", above): re-basing `TrainWeekPage`'s group cards (`WeekZoneCard`/`ZoneTrack` are deleted, §9) onto `weeklyBands`-style current→ceiling bands (and deleting `budgetOf`/`GROUP_MEV`/`FAILURE_WEEKLY_CAP`/`VOLUME_WEEKLY_CAP` once nothing calls them) is tracked as a follow-up, `mezo-d20.16`.
- **Volume exemption (budget wave-2, `mezo-0znc`; generalized from a plyo-only rule to the per-exercise flag by `mezo-gbo7`):** the shared predicate `countsForVolume(ex)` (`setBudget.ts`) — `ex.countsTowardVolume ?? ex.type !== 'plyo'`, so fixtures/plans with no flag keep the old plyo-only behaviour — decides what's **never counted toward a band or the session cap**: plyometrics (a weightless neuromuscular seed, Box/Depth Jump) and, since `mezo-gbo7`, any exercise explicitly flagged `countsTowardVolume: false` (e.g. the fix-zárás closing block). `weeklyBands` simply skips exempt sets when summing `planned`, so an **exempt-only** group never surfaces in a `WeeklyBandsCard` row at all — the per-day `daySessionBreakdown`/`DayBreakdownCard` (below) is the only surface that still reports exempt sets explicitly (`DayGroupRow.exemptSets`, kept at `sets: 0` for an exempt-only group).
- **Session cap (D3):** `sessionCapWarnings(days)` flags any day × budget-group combination whose exempt-excluded working sets (`countsForVolume`) exceed **`SESSION_MUSCLE_CAP` (8** — tightened from 11 by `mezo-d20.14`) in a single session — the fix is frequency (split the same weekly volume across ≥2 days), not shown as a number here, just the warning. (The old `wizard/StepProgram.tsx` `DayTile`s used to render this same `SESSION_MUSCLE_CAP` as their per-muscle-bar cap line; that file is **deleted** since `mezo-yty6` — the cap now surfaces on `DayLoadPanel`'s per-muscle cards.)
- **Daily breakdown card (budget wave-2, `mezo-smhn`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-03-daily-session-breakdown-design.md`, variant A):** `daySessionBreakdown(day)` (`setBudget.ts`) is the **per-day** companion to the weekly bands above — same coarse budget-group tally but scoped to the ACTIVE day only, exempt sets split out the same way via `countsForVolume` (`DayGroupRow { group, label, colorMuscle, sets, exemptSets, over }`, `over = sets > SESSION_MUSCLE_CAP`). `leastLoadedDayFor(days, group, excludeDay)` picks the best day to move sets to when a group is over the session cap — the non-off training day (excluding `excludeDay`) with the fewest counted sets of that group, ties broken by fewest total counted sets then original day order; `null` when no other training day exists. **`DayBreakdownCard`** (`components/DayBreakdownCard.tsx`) is the presentational "Ma · izmonként" card — one bar per group (fill vs. the `SESSION_MUSCLE_CAP` line, `⚠` + amber warning strip when over, suggesting `leastLoadedDayFor`'s day) — rendered nothing (`rows.length === 0`) on an empty/off day. `MesoEditor` mounts it between `MesoEditorHero` and the weekly `WeeklyBandsCard` (both levels stay visible at once — the design spec's variant B Ma/Hét switcher was rejected) and also feeds `daySessionBreakdown`'s over-groups into each `ExerciseAccordionRow`'s `highlight` prop (a red-tinted card border, `type !== 'plyo' && overGroups.has(budgetGroup(e.muscle))`).
- **Traverses `days` itself — `muscleWeek.ts` is untouched.** `setBudget.ts`/`weeklyBands.ts` run their own pass over `MesoDay[]`; neither extends `MuscleWeekRow`/`muscleWeekFromMeso` (see the design spec's §7 reality note — the logic modules stay independent, each doing its own traversal).
- **Consumers:** the unified `MesoEditor` (builder `Gyakorlatok` view via `MesoExercises`, §2 — hero, `DayBreakdownCard`, `WeeklyBandsCard`, and the accordion's over-row highlight + adaptive warmup chip, all fed from this module; the wizard's `ProgramDayView`/`StepProgram.tsx` pair used to render it too and is **deleted** since `mezo-yty6` — the wizard and the template editor both render `MesoWeekEditor`/`MesoDayEditor`, whose load surfaces are `LoadTile`/`WeekLoadPanel`/`DayLoadPanel`), and, historically, a **read-only mirror** on the now-deleted `MuscleWeekSheet`'s Gym-page detail sheet (`WeeklyBandsCard` with no `defaultOpen`/collapse concept — it always showed every row) — that mirror is gone with the sheet (T12, §2 `Heti`), and nothing on `TrainWeekPage` reads `weeklyBands`/`WeeklyBandsCard` today. `GymPage`'s meta card (now `TrainWeekPage`) no longer mirrors this planning-time module at all — the live zone unification below renders live done-vs-plan data instead.
- **Live zone unification (mid-cycle, live, `mezo-oyhy.7`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-04-live-zone-unification-design.md`) — still on the fatigue scale, follow-up `mezo-d20.16`:** a companion layer, separate from the planning-time bands above, that projects this week's ACTUAL LOGGED sets alongside the plan. `logic/weekZone.ts`'s `weekZoneRows({ plannedDays, completed, todayPlan? })` re-derives a per-budget-group `WeekZoneRow` (`doneSets`/`todaySets`/`plannedSets` + their `budgetOf` scale equivalents `doneBudget`/`todayBudget`/`planBudget`, a `zoneStart` MEV projection via `muscleBudgets`/`GROUP_MEV`, and a `status: 'below' | 'entering' | 'in' | 'over'`) from `completed: WorkoutDetailResponse[]`. Two week-outlook fields sit beside the status (`mezo-zr6p`): **`remainingPlanSets`** (`plannedSets - doneSets - todaySets`, floored at 0 — what the plan still owes) and **`setsToZone`** (`mev - max(plannedSets, doneSets + todaySets)`, floored at 0 — what's missing once the WHOLE week's plan is counted), so a `below` row can distinguish "not there **yet**" from "the week as planned never gets there" (this week's completed instances, meso + custom) — sets price by their own logged RIR (fallback: the exercise's `targetRIR`), skip-marker/warmup rows and `plyo` exercises are excluded, mirroring `setBudget.ts`'s own exclusion rules. `data/train/weekMuscleLogHooks.ts`'s **`useWeekMuscleLog()`** (barrel: `@/data/hooks`) supplies `completed`: it takes `useWeekWorkouts()`'s this-week summaries, filters `status === 'completed'`, and fires one `getWorkout` detail query per instance (`useQueries`; disabled in mock mode, so mock is always an empty week and `pending` is forced `false` there). The bar language **used to be** single-sourced in `ZoneTrack` (`components/ZoneTrack.tsx` — track + `zoneStart`-to-100% sage optimal-zone underlay + ordered `solid`/`today`/`ghost`/`overflow` segments). Its consumers went one by one — `SetBudgetCard` (`mezo-d20.14`), then `ZoneMiniGrid` with the Train Titanium T12 Terhelés reface, then `WeekZoneCard` with the prep mosaic — so `ZoneTrack`, `selectPrepRows` and `prepSegments` are **deleted** (`mezo-e1ii9` Task 7, §9); `TrainWeekPage`'s group cards read `weekZoneRows` and draw their own bars. Its `⚠ planBudget > 1` / `↓ plannedSets < mev` marks live on today under a different shape instead: `TrainWeekPage`'s group cards flag the same `planBudget > 1` condition as a `sok` chip (house amber, never a bar mark) — see the `Heti` subsection.
- **Out of scope (D4) / independence:** no integration with `VolumeProgressionService`/the volume rollover engine above — `GROUP_MEV` is a fixed client-side table, not read from the backend's per-mesocycle MEV/MAV/MRV, so `weekZone.ts`'s optimal-zone floor and the performance-driven weekly volume ramp stay two intentionally independent layers. **Partial exception, landmarks only (`mezo-3m5m`):** `weeklyBands`/`peakWeekFit` CAN read a mesocycle's own `volumePerMuscle` MEV/MAV/MRV when the caller supplies it, falling back to the static `GROUP_LANDMARKS` mirror otherwise — but neither reads `currentSets`/the live ramp state, so the independence claim holds for the RAMP itself; only the static landmark numbers have an optional live source now. Volume counting is **direct-only** by decision (ADR `docs/decisions/0021-volume-counting-direct-only.md`) — a set counts fully toward its one `budgetGroup`, never split fractionally across overlapping muscles — and fractional cross-muscle counting stays deferred behind a synergist-catalog prerequisite (bd `mezo-oyhy.5a`, backlog). Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-03-optimal-zone-bands-design.md`.
- **Structural lint — soft structural checks (`mezo-oyhy.2`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-structure-lint-design.md`):** `structureLint(days)` (`frontend/src/features/train/logic/structureLint.ts`) runs the RP/Helms/Nippard/Ethier structural consensus over the meso week: exercises/muscle/session (≤3, ham/traps ≤2), sets/exercise (compound 2–4, isolation 2–3, `countsForVolume`-exempt excluded), single-day frequency (≥4 weekly sets landing on one day), weekly variety (2–5 distinct movements per group), session size (5–9 slots, plyo counts), push:pull (silence band 0.6–1.6), ham:quad (<0.4 with quad ≥6) and rep-zone mono-diet (`mezo-oyhy.4`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-07-rep-zone-hint-design.md`: a group's dominant rep zone ≥80% of its ≥6 weekly sets — zone from the rep range, `repZoneOf` — heavy repMax ≤10 / light repMin ≥20 / else moderate; deliberate skews stay silent via `REP_ZONE_SKEW_OK` — shoulder→light, ham/glute→heavy) — thresholds live in exported constant tables (one place to tune), muscle→side map `PUSH_PULL_SIDE`. Pure derivation, findings are soft observations only — never blocking. `StructureLintCard` (`components/StructureLintCard.tsx`) renders the findings in `MesoEditor` below `WeeklyBandsCard`: collapsible, neutral `surface-2` explanation rows (label + why-detail per finding), a `{n} észrevétel` (amber wash) / `✓ rendben` (sage wash) header pill; never red, never force-opens (`useState(false)`, no `defaultOpen`), never blocks saving. The push:pull/ham:quad balance-rule copy explicitly flags its structural-balance-literature provenance (distinct from the RP-derived rules above). **Since `mezo-3m5m` (spec GD5) `structureLint` takes an optional `priorities` argument and goes silent for two rules on a Maintain group:** **R3** (single-day frequency) and **R4** (weekly variety) both `continue` past any budget group whose `tierOf(priorities, group) === 'maintain'` — a maintained group training once a week on one movement is the whole point of the tier, not a structural defect to flag. Every other rule (R1/R2/R5–R9, including the budget-adjacent `session-length`/`sets-per-exercise`) still evaluates Maintain groups exactly as before.
- **Session-length estimator — the app's single duration source (`mezo-oyhy.3`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-session-length-design.md`):** `estimateSessionMinutes(exercises)` (`frontend/src/features/train/logic/sessionLength.ts`) is a pure recipe-based duration estimate — working-rep execution (`SESSION_TIME.repSeconds` 3.5s, `plyoRepSeconds` 2s for `type==='plyo'`) × `workingSets` × avg reps, inter-set rest `restSecondsFor(type)` (compound 150s / else 90s, the live rest-timer engine's own numbers) × `(workingSets−1)`, a warm-up-set block (`warmupSetSeconds` 20s + `warmupRestSeconds` 45s) × `warmupSets`, a fixed 90s `transitionSeconds` per exercise, summed over all exercises, then **one** `Math.round` on the total plus a fixed 8-minute session warm-up block (`warmupBlockMinutes`) — 0 for an empty exercise list. It is the single formula behind every PLANNING-time duration surface: `MesoEditor`'s hero `~X perc` stat (`dayMinutes`), `structureLint`'s `session-length` rule (band `SESSION_LENGTH_BAND` 45–90 min, § above), the prep-briefing pill (`prepBriefing.ts`'s `prepStats().durationEst` — both module and pill are deleted since `mezo-e1ii9`), and the `TrainTodayPage` gym-hero chip (`workoutMinutes`). **Corrections to two long-stale claims here (mezo-1jm8):** (1) two live UI call sites DO read the API's `durationEst` field, both passing it into `WorkoutSummary`'s `durationMin` prop as the "terv" (planned) half of the summary line — `WorkoutReviewPage.tsx` (`detail.durationEst ?? null`, the done-day review) and `ActiveWorkoutPage.tsx` (`W.durationEst`, the in-session summary phase); the previous "no UI reads it" claim only *looked* true because the column is always NULL in real mode (below), so the read never visibly did anything. (2) The `TodayPage` `gymMinutes` consumer this used to name **no longer exists** — `TodayPage` and its whole view layer were retired outright (`937a6c090`, `mezo-d20.9.1`, superseded by the Mai/`NapHubPage` surfaces), and nothing replaced that particular consumer; `gymMinutes` has zero references left in `frontend/src`. **`workout_session.duration_est` has no writer at all, in production or otherwise** (undocumented until now): the only assignment anywhere in the backend is the template→instance copy in `WorkoutService.startWorkout` (`instance.setDurationEst(template.getDurationEst())`, `WorkoutService.java:534`) — nothing ever populates a *template* row's `duration_est`, so every template (and therefore every instance, and therefore every `durationEst` the two live call sites above receive) is permanently `NULL` in production; `WorkoutSummary` degrades gracefully (`durationMin` falsy → no "terv" clause), so the gap is silent rather than broken. This is why the estimator above, not the DB column, is the app's real duration source. Open bug: **`mezo-lhmh`** (P3) — the gym push-notification title (`AnchorResolver.gymTitle`, `AnchorResolver.java:203`) reads this same always-NULL column and therefore always falls back to the bare day label instead of `"{durationEst} perc: {dayLabel}"`; the fix is either to start populating a template's `duration_est` or to switch that title to the `mezo-2k4q.2` timing profile once it exists.
- **Peak-week session-time fit signal (`mezo-3m5m`, spec GD6) — a DISTINCT surface from R8 above:** `peakWeekFit(days, priorities?, volumePerMuscle?)` (`frontend/src/features/train/logic/peakWeekFit.ts`) projects each budget group's counted exercises onto its **CURRENT tier's landmark target** (same `tierTargetOf`/`GROUP_LANDMARKS` resolution as `muscleBudgets` above — i.e. what the week would look like once every group actually reaches its tier's weekly set target, not what it looks like today) via `projectGroup`, a pure FRONTEND MIRROR of the backend's `WorkoutService.effectiveWorkingSets` (`backend/.../WorkoutService.java:380-441`: a floor of 1 counted set per exercise, the remainder handed out proportional to each exercise's template `workingSets`, largest-fractional-remainder tie-break), then re-estimates each day's session length (`estimateSessionMinutes`, § above) against that PROJECTED plan and returns only the training days that fall outside `SESSION_LENGTH_BAND` (45–90 min) — a plan that fits its own peak week everywhere yields `[]`. **`PeakFitCard`** (`components/PeakFitCard.tsx`, "Csúcshét · időbecslés") renders those days ("Kedd: csúcshéten ~112 perc — vegyél el, vagy tedd át." / "…csúcshéten is csak ~30 perc — férne még bele inger."), modeled on `StructureLintCard`'s soft-grey/never-red/never-force-opens register, mounted in `MesoEditor` above `StructureLintCard`; renders `null` when there is nothing to flag. **R8 reads the template's OWN minutes today; `PeakFitCard` reads the projected PEAK week's** — neither merges into nor silences the other, and a plan can be R8-clean today while still flagging here (a group ramping toward Emphasize's MRV will add sets it doesn't have yet).
- **Generator fitter — RETIRED (`mezo-oyhy.6`'s `generateProgram`/`fitProgram`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-07-generator-fitter-design.md`, deleted whole by `mezo-d20.14`).** The client-side generator `frontend/src/features/train/logic/planner.ts` (goal-preset `SCHEMES`, curated `SPLIT_TEMPLATES`) and its three-phase rep-zone/volume/session-length fitter `frontend/src/features/train/logic/programFit.ts` (`fitProgram`), together with their calibrated invariant suite `planner.test.ts`, no longer exist — the wizard's `Program` step sources its plan exclusively from the backend-mirrored **plan generator** (`POST /api/train/meso-plans/generate`, §4 `#### Plan generator` above), whose own `MesoPlanSkeleton`/`MesoPlanFiller`/`MesoPlanMerger` pipeline (backend) and `logic/mesoPlan.ts`/`data/train/mesoPlanMock.ts` (FE mock twin) took over split-derivation, week-1/ceiling set math and exercise fill; there is no client-side fit-after-generate pass any more — the generator's split table guarantees ≥2×/week per group by construction (asserted server-side in `MesoPlanSkeletonTest`), so no removal/trim engine is needed. **`SESSION_MUSCLE_CAP` is NOT such a guarantee — it is a display/lint threshold, not an invariant:** `dayFrames`/`MesoPlanSkeleton` spread a group's week-1 sets over its ≥2 training days, which keeps the DEFAULT `GROUP_LANDMARKS` week-1 numbers under the cap on every 2–6-day split, but a high per-mesocycle `volumePerMuscle` override (or any hand edit) can push a single session over it — and is then **flagged** by `sessionCapWarnings`/the red tab dot/the day tiles' cap badge, never prevented. `structureLint`/`peakWeekFit`/`estimateSessionMinutes` (below) are unaffected — they still evaluate whatever program is currently in the wizard's/builder's draft, generated or hand-edited.

#### Actual-duration measurement — slice 1 (`mezo-1jm8`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-02-workout-timing-design.md`)
Slice 1 of the workout-timing epic adds a MEASURED counterpart to the estimator above, sitting entirely alongside it — nothing about `estimateSessionMinutes`/`durationEst` changed. Two clocks reach the DB, deliberately kept separate (the Strava pattern): the RAW wall-clock pair `started_at`/`finished_at` (`202609021400_mezo-1jm8_workout_session_timing.sql`, both nullable `TIMESTAMPTZ` on `workout_session`) and a DERIVED `active_seconds` (nullable `INTEGER`) with dead air trimmed out. Elapsed wall-clock time is always `finished_at − started_at` and is never itself stored.
- **Stamping (`WorkoutService.java`):** `started_at` is stamped **once**, in `startWorkout`'s new-instance branch only (`instance.setStartedAt(Instant.now())`) — the resume branch above it returns earlier, so a mid-workout reload or app restart can never restart the clock. `finished_at` is filled **if-empty** at `finishWorkout` (same idempotent pattern as the closing-note fill), so a retried finish call never moves the wall clock. Immediately after, `activeSeconds` is derived from the instance's own non-skipped logged sets' `done_at` values and `SessionTimingCalculator.activeSeconds` — **wrapped in try/catch**: timing is derived/decorative, so a failure there is logged (`log.warn`) and swallowed rather than rolling back the user's real write (the finish itself, already committed above it).
- **`SessionTimingCalculator`** (pure, static, Spring-free — `feature/train/service/SessionTimingCalculator.java`, unit-tested via `SessionTimingCalculatorTest`): sorts the session's `done_at` stamps, sums each **consecutive pair's** delta (each independently clipped to `[0, gapCapSeconds]` — "rest + the next set's execution" is treated as one indivisible unit), plus a separately-clipped **lead-in** term (`started_at` → the first `done_at`, clipped to `[0, leadInCapSeconds]`, added only when `started_at` is present). Returns `null` for an empty/absent `done_at` list — "unknown", never a fabricated `0`.
- **Abandoned-session discriminator:** `WorkoutAutoCloseService` settles a stale `active` instance to `completed`/`skipped` exactly as before (§4 above) but **deliberately never stamps `finished_at`** — so `status = 'completed' AND finished_at IS NULL` identifies a session the auto-close closed on the user's behalf, not one they actually finished, without needing a new column.
- **Backfill** (same migration, idempotent — only fills rows where `active_seconds IS NULL AND status = 'completed'`): reconstructs history from `exercise_set.done_at`, which has always been written. Per session, consecutive non-deleted/non-skipped `done_at` deltas (`lag() over (partition by workout_session_id order by done_at)`) are each clipped at 300s and summed; there is no lead-in term (`started_at` does not exist on historical rows). **A Postgres discovery worth flagging for future SQL in this area:** `least()`/`greatest()` **ignore a NULL argument rather than propagating it** (unlike a plain comparison) — so `least(NULL, 300)` evaluates to `300`, not `NULL`. Every session's first ordered set has a NULL delta (`lag()` has nothing to look back to), and clipping it naively would have credited every backfilled session with a bogus 300s phantom gap. The migration filters `where delta is not null` **before** the `least()` clip, not after, specifically to dodge this. One consequence documented in the migration and covered by `WorkoutTimingBackfillIT`: a session with exactly one logged set produces no delta row at all, so the update's join finds nothing and `active_seconds` correctly stays `NULL` ("unknown"), not `0`.
- **Tunables — `mezo.train.timing.*` → `TimingProperties`** (`feature/train/config/TimingProperties.java`, `@Validated` record; values in `application.yml`):

  | Property | Default | Used by |
  |---|---|---|
  | `gap-cap-seconds` | 300 | ✅ slice 1 — inter-set clip; also slice 2's per-interval drop threshold (`TimingObservationExtractor`) |
  | `lead-in-cap-seconds` | 900 | ✅ slice 1 — start→first-set clip; also slice 2's `lead_in` observation cap |
  | `max-clipped-ratio` | 0.25 | ✅ slice 2 — the Karn `tooNoisy` session-exclusion threshold (below) |
  | `alpha` | 0.125 | ✅ slice 2 (RFC 6298 EWMA weight, `EwmaEstimator.update`) |
  | `beta` | 0.25 | ✅ slice 2 (RFC 6298 EWMA weight, `EwmaEstimator.update`) |
  | `outlier-k` | 4 | ✅ slice 2 — the outlier-gate width, in deviations |
  | `min-samples` | 3 | ✅ slice 2 — the outlier gate stays open below this |
  | `min-deviation-seconds` | 20 | ✅ slice 2 — the outlier gate's RFC 6298 granularity floor (below) |
  | `seed-set-cycle-compound` | 180 | ✅ slice 2 — cold-start seed |
  | `seed-set-cycle-isolation` | 125 | ✅ slice 2 — cold-start seed |
  | `seed-transition` | 240 | ✅ slice 2 — cold-start seed |
  | `seed-lead-in` | 480 | ✅ slice 2 — cold-start seed |

  All eight slice-2-only tunables (`max-clipped-ratio` through `seed-lead-in`) are the backend's own copy of the frontend's static pacing constants (`SESSION_TIME` + `restSecondsFor`) plus the learner's own gate math — see the `#### Learned workout-timing profile — slice 2` section below for how each is actually used; they sat unused in slice 1 (`mezo-1jm8`) before slice 2 (`mezo-dzbm`) shipped the code that reads them.
- **Contract** (`api/feature/train/train.yml`): `WorkoutInstanceResponse` and `WorkoutDetailResponse` both gained `startedAt`/`finishedAt` (nullable date-time, absent on pre-`mezo-1jm8` rows) and `activeSeconds` (nullable integer, absent when nothing was logged); `ExerciseSetResponse` gained `doneAt`.
- **Frontend consumption (`logic/actualDuration.ts`):** `actualMinutes(t: SessionTiming)` picks the number to show — elapsed wall clock (`finishedAt − startedAt`) when both are present, else `activeSeconds`, else `null`; either way, a result under 60 seconds also returns `null` (nothing meaningful to show). `WorkoutSummary` gained an `actualMin?: number | null` prop rendered alongside the existing `durationMin` (the estimate) as a "terv ~X tény Y perc" pairing when both exist, `tény`-only when just `actualMin` exists, `~X perc`-only when just `durationMin` exists. The combined case originally forced a `<br/>` between the two halves (measured to overflow a 360px-wide phone otherwise, `61ee0d777`), which fixed the overflow but also forced two lines at every width, including ones where the pairing fits on one line; the deferred cleanup in slice 2 (`mezo-dzbm`) replaced it with a non-breaking space between `actualMin` and `perc` — the browser wraps normally between words again, and only the mid-word "71"/"perc" split that caused the original overflow stays prevented. Re-measured live (mock fixture `wd-mock-1`, `terv ~62 tény 71 perc`): at 360px the line wraps to two lines with no overflow (`scrollWidth === clientWidth === 292px`), at 414px it renders on one line. `WorkoutReviewPage` wires `actualMin={actualMinutes(detail)}` off the persisted `WorkoutDetailResponse` — the authoritative, post-finish surface this feature is for. `ActiveWorkoutPage` passed **no measured minutes** through T7 (`actualMin={null}`, then the closing ceremony's `minutes={null}`), on the rationale that the backend only ever writes `activeSeconds` inside `finishWorkout`, never on the start/active path, and this page never refetches `/today` after finishing — so its held `open` instance can never carry a real measurement, and passing anything else would silently show stale/undefined data. **That reasoning covered the wrong object, and `mezo-e1ii9` Task 3 fixed it — see below.**

**The closing ceremony is TWO screens (`mezo-e1ii9`, Train parity P1 Task 3).** 1:1 with the prototype's `session.js` (`summary()` → `detailsStep()`): step one is the ignition + the reading (eyebrow, five-star row, comet bar, the three counters, the sr-only star heading, the verdict, the `perc`/`XP` stat tiles, the optional `Új rekord` row, the pending-sets line) ending in exactly ONE way on — `Részletek · Izomcsoportok és a nyert kalória`; step two is the per-muscle star rows, the kcal tile, the closing note, `Vissza a mai napra` and `Vissza az értékeléshez` back to step one. T7 had collapsed both onto one screen citing the ceremony-pattern doc's "two acts"; acts are choreography *inside* a screen, while the `Részletek` step is a screen — [the doc is corrected](../design_2.0/2026-09-15-ceremony-pattern.md). Two things T7 carried into the ceremony are **gone**, because the prototype's close has no counterpart for either: the **küldetés rows** (their remaining home is the review page's `Kihívások` strip, pinned by a `WorkoutReviewPage` test) and the mock-mode **streak / level-up toast** (`awardGamificationEvent(…, { silent: true })` — the ceremony *is* the reward surface and owns the screen alone). The one-shot rAF pass, its `prefers-reduced-motion` branch and its ref guard are untouched: a trip to step two and back never replays it, and leaving step one mid-pass still brings the FINAL numbers back.

**The ceremony's `a pulton töltött idő` tile (same slice) — now really measured.** The rationale above was about the page's *held* `open` instance, which is indeed always stale. But the **finish POST's own response** is a different object and it *is* authoritative: `WorkoutService.finishWorkout` fills `finishedAt`, derives `activeSeconds`, and returns all three through `toInstanceResponse`, so `WorkoutInstanceResponse` comes back measured. `ActiveWorkoutPage` therefore captures `{startedAt, finishedAt, activeSeconds}` in `finishAndCelebrate`'s `onSuccess` (`finishTiming`) and feeds `minutes={actualMinutes(finishTiming)}` to `WorkoutCeremony` — **the real clock, with no extra round trip and no refetch**. `actualMinutes` still returns `null` for anything unmeasurable (no `startedAt` on a pre-`mezo-1jm8` row, a derivation that threw, or a session under 60 seconds), and a `null` still renders **no tile at all**: the prototype always shows the stat, but an estimate is never printed as if it were a measurement. **Mock mode** has no wire response (the finish mutation is a local no-op), so the page falls back to its own mount-to-finish wall clock — still a measurement, not an estimate, because since Task 1 entering the route *is* the start of the session.
- **Tests:** `SessionTimingCalculatorTest` (unit, the pure calculator), `WorkoutTimingIT` (stamping + derivation through `startWorkout`/`finishWorkout`), `WorkoutTimingBackfillIT` (migration idempotency + the single-set NULL case), `actualDuration.test.ts` (FE, the elapsed-vs-active fallback + the 60s floor).

#### Learned workout-timing profile — slice 2 (`mezo-dzbm`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-02-workout-timing-design.md`)
Slice 2 adds a per-user LEARNED counterpart to the static `estimateSessionMinutes` recipe above, calibrating (not replacing) it from the actual-duration measurements slice 1 produces. `workout_timing_profile` (`202609021600_mezo-dzbm_create_workout_timing_profile.sql`) holds one row per user per **component**, each an RFC 6298 SRTT/RTTVAR pair: `value_num` (smoothed estimate, seconds), `deviation_num` (smoothed absolute deviation, the outlier gate's width), `samples`.

- **The four components, and what interval each one measures** (`TimingObservationExtractor`, pure/static/Spring-free, unit-tested via `TimingObservationExtractorTest`). `done_at` marks a set's **completion**, not its start, so a completed session's sorted `done_at` stream only ever exposes "gap between two completions" as an observable unit — **rest and the next set's execution are never separated**, because separating them would require capturing a set's *start* too, which is new capture UI slice 2 deliberately did not add. The extractor buckets each consecutive-pair gap by what it crosses:
  - `lead_in` — `started_at` → the first `done_at` (the session's warm-up block), added only when `started_at` is present and the gap is within `leadInCapSeconds`.
  - `set_cycle_compound` / `set_cycle_isolation` — two consecutive `done_at`s on the **same exercise**, bucketed by that exercise's `type` (`compound` vs everything else) to mirror the frontend's `restSecondsFor` split.
  - `transition` — a gap crossing an exercise boundary: exercise A's last set → exercise B's first set (rest + changeover + B's first-set execution, as one interval).

  **Warm-up sets count as ordinary sets on both sides of the loop.** The extractor does not special-case a `kind: 'warmup'` set — its `done_at` feeds the same consecutive-pair logic as any working set — and `estimateSessionMinutes`'s profile-calibrated path sums over every exercise's sets the same way. Measurement and estimate therefore share one decomposition; if warm-ups were measured one way and estimated another, the learned numbers would calibrate against a shape the estimate never reproduces, making them meaningless.

- **The cold start and the dropped `global_multiplier` component.** Seeds live in config (`mezo.train.timing.seed-set-cycle-compound` 180, `seed-set-cycle-isolation` 125, `seed-transition` 240, `seed-lead-in` 480 → `TimingProperties`) — an untuned starting point, hand-picked from the frontend's static pacing constants (`SESSION_TIME` + `restSecondsFor`) but deliberately **not** calibrated to reproduce them. `TimingProfileService.read` always returns all four components, config seeds filling in any the user has not yet accumulated data for (`EwmaEstimator.seed`, `deviation = value / 2`, `samples = 0`), so the client never implements a cold-start branch — but a brand-new profile does **not** return the static formula's numbers: the calibrated path charges every warm-up set a full `set_cycle` (the extractor does not distinguish a warm-up's `done_at` from a working set's) where the static formula prices a warm-up far cheaper, and prices a changeover at `seedTransition` (240s) vs the static formula's 90s. A realistic 5-exercise day computes to noticeably more minutes on the seeded/calibrated path than on the static formula (see `sessionLength.test.ts` and `TrainTodayPage.test.tsx` for the exact numbers) — see the consequence note below. The design spec originally planned a fifth component, `global_multiplier` (session active-seconds ÷ the formula's estimate, applied as a blanket scale-up on top of the four interval components) specifically to cover the gap before any interval component had `minSamples` observations. It was **dropped during planning**, for a reason that has nothing to do with the seeds matching the static formula: computing it on the backend would have meant porting `estimateSessionMinutes`'s whole recipe (rep/rest/warm-up/transition arithmetic) from TypeScript into Java just to have a denominator to divide by — a second, duplicated copy of the same formula with a permanent drift hazard between the two — for a gap that the per-interval components already close within a session or two of real observations (each component gates independently at `minSamples`).

- **Known consequence of the untuned seeds (outstanding work).** Until the seeds above are retuned against real observation data, the calibrated surfaces — `TrainTodayPage`'s gym-hero chip and `MesoEditor`'s `dayMinutes` hero stat — read noticeably higher than the static formula. `structureLint`'s `session-length` rule still judges the static formula against the fixed 45–90 minute `SESSION_LENGTH_BAND` (see below), so the hero stat and the lint card on the same screen can disagree: a day the calibrated hero reports as running long can still show green on the static-formula lint check, and vice versa. This is a known, accepted gap — not a bug to fix by retuning the seeds now, which must instead be done against real usage data.

- **The Karn exclusions — a session is trusted whole or not at all** (`TimingProfileService.learnFrom`). Two cases skip the ENTIRE session rather than partially learning from it: (1) an **auto-closed** session — `finished_at IS NULL` while `status = 'completed'` (the `WorkoutAutoCloseService` marker from slice 1, §above) — its provenance is ambiguous, the user never actually confirmed it was done; (2) a **`tooNoisy`** session — `TimingObservationExtractor.Result.tooNoisy(maxClippedRatio)`, true when the share of gaps dropped for exceeding `gapCapSeconds` (or being ≤0) exceeds `mezo.train.timing.max-clipped-ratio` (0.25). Both checks happen before any component is touched, so a noisy or ambiguous session contributes zero observations, not a partially-trusted subset.

- **The `minDeviationSeconds` gate floor** (`EwmaEstimator.update`, unit-tested via `EwmaEstimatorTest`). An observation only updates the estimate if it lies within `outlierK` (4) smoothed deviations of the current value — otherwise it is **dropped, not clipped** (clipping a contaminated sample would bias the estimate upward permanently). The gate compares against `max(minDeviationSeconds, current.deviation())`, not `current.deviation()` alone: without the floor, a user with near-identical intervals converges `deviation` toward zero, and once `samples >= minSamples` an unfloored gate of `outlierK * 0` rejects **every** subsequent observation, freezing the profile permanently and silently — the exact failure mode RFC 6298 itself floors `RTTVAR` against for the same reason. The floor applies to the **gate comparison only** — the stored `deviation_num` itself is never floored, so it stays a faithful measurement of the user's actual consistency (a very consistent user's stored deviation can still read below the floor; only the *decision to admit or reject* is floored).

- **Architecture — why learning runs AFTER `finishWorkout` commits, on its own thread** (`TimingProfileListener`, `WorkoutFinishedEvent`). `WorkoutService.finishWorkout` publishes `WorkoutFinishedEvent` unconditionally at the end of its own transaction (mirrors `ChatService`/`ChatTurnCompleted`); `TimingProfileListener.onWorkoutFinished` is `@TransactionalEventListener(phase = AFTER_COMMIT)` **and** `@Async`, calling `TimingProfileService.learnFrom` inside a try/catch that logs and swallows any `RuntimeException`. Two earlier shapes were tried and rejected:
  - **`Propagation.NESTED`** (learning inside a savepoint of `finishWorkout`'s own transaction) silently dropped every `apply()` write — the savepoint never actually persisted the profile rows, with no exception raised to say so. This was the slice's first real bug (found via a `duplicate key` constraint violation in an early IT run, `task-10-report.md`).
  - **A joined `REQUIRED` transaction** (calling `learnFrom` directly from inside `finishWorkout`, guarded only by a try/catch in the caller) is unsafe for a different reason: Spring marks a PARTICIPATING transaction rollback-only the instant any exception escapes a joined `@Transactional` callee, regardless of whether that callee touched the database — so a bug in profile learning would take the user's already-committed-in-intent completed workout down with it, and the caller's try/catch cannot stop a rollback-only flag from being set.

  `@Async` is **load-bearing, not decorative**: without it, the `AFTER_COMMIT` callback runs synchronously on the thread that just committed, with that transaction's connection and persistence context still bound. `TimingProfileService.learnFrom`'s plain `@Transactional` (default `REQUIRED`) would then **join** the already-committed transaction instead of opening a fresh one, and every write inside `apply()` would be silently dropped — committed nowhere, because there is no longer an active transaction to commit. `@Async` detaches the listener onto a thread with no ambient transaction, so `REQUIRED` there genuinely opens a new one. `TimingProfileService.apply` also uses `saveAndFlush`, not `save`, so a constraint violation surfaces synchronously inside the listener's own try/catch rather than at a deferred flush nobody is watching.

- **The feature switch (`mezo.feature.timing-profile.enabled` → `FeaturesConfiguration.TIMING_PROFILE_SWITCH`) gates the WRITE path only.** `TimingProfileListener` is `@ConditionalOnProperty` on the switch — off ⇒ the bean does not exist ⇒ `WorkoutFinishedEvent` has no listener ⇒ nothing new is ever learned. `TrainController.getTimingProfile` and `TimingProfileService.read` carry **no such gate** — this is deliberate, not an oversight: the frontend's `useTimingProfile()` call sites never need to branch on the switch being on or off; they only ever see "a complete profile." For a user who has never learned anything, switch-off reads exactly as the cold-start response (the config seeds). But `read` is ungated and simply returns whatever rows exist — toggling the switch off **after** a user has already learned does **not** revert them to seeds; their previously-learned values keep being served (and stay frozen, since no new learning happens while the switch is off).

- **Which estimate consumers receive the profile, and which deliberately do not** (`estimateSessionMinutes(exercises, profile?)`, `logic/sessionLength.ts` — the optional second parameter is backward-compatible, so only 2 of the function's 7 call sites were changed: `programFit.ts` has two call sites of its own, `:104` and `:153`, both still on the static path). **Receive it:** `TrainTodayPage`'s gym-hero `workoutMinutes` chip and `MesoEditor`'s `dayMinutes` hero stat — both are "how long will THIS session take ME" predictions shown to the user who generated the data. `MesoEditor` is presentational and never calls `useTimingProfile()` itself; it takes `timingProfile`/`timingProfilePending` as props, wired identically from its three parent pages (`MesocycleBuilderPage` via `MesoExercises`, `MesoTemplateEditorPage`, `MesocyclePlannerPage`), each of which owns its own `useTimingProfile()` call. **Deliberately do NOT:** `structureLint`'s `session-length` rule (the 45–90 minute `SESSION_LENGTH_BAND`), `peakWeekFit` and `programFit` all stay on the static formula (`prepBriefing`'s duration pill was a fourth, until the prep screen and the module were deleted, §9). These encode **programming rules**, not personal predictions: a per-user calibrated number would drift the rule's meaning out from under its own fixed thresholds — a slow-paced but well-designed session would start tripping `structureLint`'s overrun warning purely because of who is running it, and the band's meaning would no longer be comparable across users.
- **Tests:** `EwmaEstimatorTest` (unit, the outlier gate + the deviation floor), `TimingObservationExtractorTest` (unit, the four-component decomposition + `tooNoisy`), `TimingProfileIT` (learning through `finishWorkout` → `AFTER_COMMIT` → the persisted profile, the Karn exclusions), `TimingProfileSwitchOffIT` (switch off ⇒ no learning, read still returns seeds), `sessionLength.test.ts` (FE, the profile-calibrated path vs the static path).

#### Closing block — fix zárás (`mezo-z2ul`)
`ClosingBlockService` (`feature/train/service/ClosingBlockService.java`) **lazily ensures the configured closing exercises at the END of every non-empty template day of the active meso** — by default **Dead Hang** (2 × 45–60 mp; weightless `plyo` type → reps-are-seconds, kg-stepper hidden on the FE) and **45° Back Extension** (2 × 12–15). Triggered from `WorkoutService.getToday` behind the `ClosingBlockGate` `@ConditionalOnProperty` marker bean (`mezo.feature.closing-block.enabled`; key constant `FeaturesConfiguration.CLOSING_BLOCK_SWITCH`; mirrors `HypertrophyDriveGate`). **Idempotent** by catalog id OR case-insensitive name (a manually added copy counts, nothing duplicates); **empty (rest) template days are skipped** so the block never turns a rest day into a workout; a day-edit that strips the rows self-heals on the next `getToday`. Config: `mezo.closing-block.exercises` — `ClosingBlockProperties` (`feature/train/config/`), a `@Validated` record list of `{slug, workingSets, repMin, repMax, targetRir}` where `slug` references a **master catalog row** (a missing slug logs a warning and is skipped, never a 500). The appended rows are **ordinary catalog-linked template exercises** (`warmupSets 0`, server-side `createdBy`), so logging, records, history and prescriptions work unchanged — **no API contract change, no DDL** (the new `dead-hang` master entry ships via `content/exercise-catalog.json`; `back-extension-45` already existed). ITs: `ClosingBlockIT` (append/idempotency/multi-day/rest-day/manual-duplicate) + `ClosingBlockSwitchOffIT`.

#### Lazy auto-close of stale instances (`mezo-cd8s`)
`WorkoutAutoCloseService` (`feature/train/service/WorkoutAutoCloseService.java`) **settles abandoned workout instances** so a session left open on a past day never lingers as `active`. Invoked as the **first statement** of `WorkoutService.getToday` (before the closing-block ensure and the meso lookup, so stale instances settle even when there is no active meso).
